- A seam between 25 and 35 degrees (a gentle bevel split in UV) now reads one side or the other by the 30 degree line;
  not looked at on real data.
- Not committed.

## Session 61: Hand-Off, Material Channels in the Master (Decisions 79 to 83)

- **Date:** 20261118
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5 (the hand-off; executed by a sub-agent, which appends Session 62)
- **Base Commit SHA:** f29d62e (Sessions 56 to 60, `fss_blender_tools` 0.103.0), plus another session's uncommitted
  UV packer work in the same tree (not this task's; see Do Not)

### Why

The maintainer asked to bake more than AO into the master: the object's albedo, roughness, metal and emissive, from
whatever its material is at the time of the bake (a high-resolution texture assigned for the bake only, or a changed
colour or value), so that Apply writes them into the texture set's textures in whatever UV layout the set has later.
Emissive is also to get, later, the light the object receives in the scene.

### Maintainer Decisions

79. **The artist chooses the channels of each bake.** A bake bakes the chosen channels (AO, Albedo, Roughness, Metal,
    Emissive) of the master from the object as it is, and overwrites them; the master's other channels stay as they
    are. Curvature and the denoise go with AO, as today.
80. **A material channel goes stale only when the mesh changes,** as AO does; not when the material or the
    surroundings change (putting the material back after the bake is the workflow). Missing and Stale bakes only the
    chosen channels, so a stale albedo is baked again only when Albedo is chosen (the artist puts its material back
    first).
81. **A bake keeps the master's layout while the mesh is unchanged,** for every channel, AO included, whatever the
    texture set's UVs or size are now. A new layout is made only when the mesh changed or there is no master; it
    removes the master's channels the bake does not bake (they no longer fit). After a texture is made larger, masters
    keep their detail until a new layout; Apply already counts the faces whose master is under 4 x their density.
82. **Apply's targets:** albedo into the texture of the material's base colour slot, roughness and metal into the
    green and blue of the ORM texture (AO stays in red), emissive into the texture of the emissive slot. Faces without
    an up-to-date master of a channel keep their pixels in that channel.
83. **Order:** the material channels first (this hand-off). The emissive from the light the object receives is a
    later, separate hand-off.

### The Rule

1. **What a material channel bakes** (the material of the texture set, as Cycles shades it, through the material's own
   UV maps, images, vertex colours and attributes): the value reaching the Principled BSDF that feeds the Material
   Output's Surface.
   - Albedo: the Base Color socket's input. When that is the output of a node whose group is `.Apply AO Group` (the
     MSFS material multiplies the base colour by the occlusion there), the group node's first input instead, so the
     AO is never in the albedo.
   - Roughness, Metal: the Roughness and Metallic sockets' inputs.
   - Emissive: the Emission Color socket's input (the MSFS emissive texture times its factor); not Emission Strength,
     which `msfs_emissive_scale` drives and stays a material value.
   - An unlinked socket bakes its value. A material without such a Principled BSDF: that channel is skipped for the
     texture set, said in the log and the report.
2. **How:** in the bake's Blender (the snapshot), a temporary copy of the target's evaluated mesh with its materials
   and every UV map and attribute, plus the master's UV as a new map, set active for the bake while the active render
   UV stays the mesh's own (Image Texture nodes without a UV Map node read the render UV); the material's socket
   source routed to an Emission shader on the Material Output, on a temporary copy of the material; Cycles bake
   `EMIT` into the master's tiles and bands (the core and the skirt, as AO), then the composition as AO's. No
   occluders, no poses, no copies' views, no denoise. Samples: a few, to average each texel; measure and report how
   many give values stable within 1/65535 on a texture of the test.
3. **Stored:** one single-channel 16-bit image per component in the master's format (lossless JPEG XL), the kinds
   `albedo_r`, `albedo_g`, `albedo_b`, `rough`, `metal`, `emis_r`, `emis_g`, `emis_b`, beside `<stem>.ao.jxl` and
   sharing its layout (`.ao.npz`, `.ao.map`), so `open_master(kind=...)` and the C++ copy read them unchanged. Albedo
   and emissive stored sRGB-encoded (as their textures), roughness and metal linear. No C++ change.
4. **The index:** the master's entry gets `channels: {group: {mesh_hash, baked_at, samples, object}}` for the groups
   `albedo`, `rough`, `metal`, `emis`. A grade per channel (`UP_TO_DATE`, `MESH`, `MISSING`): `MESH` when the
   channel's `mesh_hash` is not the item's, `MISSING` when a file is missing. AO keeps its grades.
5. **The layout (decision 81):** a bake of any channel whose master exists with the item's mesh hash reads the layout
   from `.ao.npz` and `.ao.map` instead of `build_master` (the AO baked into it as well); otherwise it builds a new
   layout and deletes the master's files of the channels it does not bake, their `channels` entries and, when AO is
   not baked, the AO's image and its curvature (AO then grades `MISSING`). A master with material channels and no AO
   is a valid master for those channels.
6. **Shared meshes** (proposal, following decision 77; the maintainer may change it at the review): the material
   channels of a shared mesh are baked from one copy, chosen as decision 77's bake choice (the drop-down, else the
   active object, else the first selected copy by name; without a selected copy the first by name), whatever its
   combine; the entry's `channels` records the copy.
7. **The panel:** in the bake box, a row of toggles (AO, Albedo, Roughness, Metal, Emissive; AO alone by default)
   that every bake button uses (Bake Selected, Missing and Stale, Selected All, and the bake of Bake and Apply). The
   Object box lists the material channels its master has, one short line each with its grade. In the Apply box, a
   row of the same toggles (AO alone by default) choosing what Apply writes. No other new text.
8. **Apply (decision 82):** per chosen channel, the copy as AO's (`copy_master`, one `copy_context` shared by a
   texture set's channels), with the same padding, per-object rule, bands and Target:
   - Target in place: the slot's texture (ORM, base colour, emissive), its other channels and alpha byte for byte; a
     copy of the file kept in the bake folder before the first write, as for the ORM; an 8-bit file written 16-bit
     with its values kept, as for the ORM.
   - Target File: `<material>.albedo.png` and `<material>.emissive.png` (RGB) in the output folder; roughness and
     metal into the green and blue of the ORM-layout file (Channels ORM).
   - Faces without an up-to-date master of the channel keep their pixels in it; listed as AO's missing faces are.
   AO in Apply is unchanged.

### Read First

- This journal's Sessions 15, 30 to 33 (the master, tiles and bands), 37, 54 to 56 (curvature, denoise, the
  decision 77 hand-off), 60; AGENTS.md; `tests/README.md`.
- `fss_blender_tools/ao_bake_runner.py` (its docstring, `bake_item`, `_same_layout_curvature`, the index entry),
  `ao_bake_core.py` (`build_master`, `save_master`, `load_master`, `open_master`, `image_paths`, `all_master_files`,
  `copy_master`, `copy_context`), `ao_bake_store.py` (`refresh`, `grade`, `curv_grade`, `make_job`),
  `ao_bake_apply.py`, `ao_bake.py` (the settings, `bake_instances`, `_draw_object`, the bake and Apply boxes,
  `FSS_OT_AOBakeStart`).
- `io_scene_gltf2_msfs_fss/blender/material/msfs_material.py` (how the MSFS material links its groups to the
  Principled BSDF), `fss_blender_tools/texture_sets.py` (`P_BASE`, `P_ORM`), `materials.py` (the emissive slot).

### Steps

1. Build the rule (items 1 to 8).
2. Tests in `tests/test_ao_bake.py`, `test_material_channels`, on a synthetic scene (Principled materials built in the
   script; one with a node group named `.Apply AO Group` multiplying the colour by 0.5):
   1. Constant values (base colour, roughness 0.3, metal 1, an emission colour): bake the four channels, Apply into
      textures of the same UVs: every face pixel equal to the value within 1/65535 after the sRGB step.
   2. A textured base colour (an 8-bit image in its own UV map): bake Albedo, put a plain grey material back,
      Refresh: Albedo `UP_TO_DATE`; Apply: report the difference to the source texture (mean, maximum, the share of
      pixels within 1/255). A measurement, no pass line.
   3. The texture set repacked (its UVs moved and scaled, the density changed), AO alone baked: the layout files
      byte-identical, the albedo files byte-identical, Albedo `UP_TO_DATE`; Apply into the repacked UVs: the same
      measurement as 2.
   4. The mesh edited: every channel `MESH`; Albedo alone baked: a new layout, the AO image and curvature removed, AO
      `MISSING`.
   5. AO alone baked with the material changed: the material channels' files byte-identical.
   6. The `.Apply AO Group` material: the baked albedo is the colour before the group (not halved).
   7. Two copies of a shared mesh with object-linked materials of different colours: the chosen copy's colour baked
      (each of the drop-down, active and first-by-name cases).
   8. Apply Albedo on objects of which one has no albedo master: its faces keep their pixels byte for byte.
   9. A material without a Principled BSDF: the channel skipped and reported.
3. `tests/test_panels.py`: the toggles in the bake and Apply boxes; the Object box's channel lines only for channels a
   master has.
4. The suites: the whole `tests/test_ao_bake.py` (its reference values and scene-rule reference must stay equal),
   `tests/test_ao_copy_native.py`, `tests/test_ao_bake_native.py`, `tests/test_ao_bake_native_job.py`,
   `tests/test_panels.py`.
5. Append Session 62 to this journal: what was built, each finish line with its numbers (MET or NOT MET), the
   measurements of steps 2.2 and 2.3 and the samples chosen, what was not verified. Do not commit.

### Finish Lines

| # | Line | How it is checked |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | The chosen channels are baked and overwritten; the others stay byte-identical | steps 2.1, 2.5 |
| 2 | A material channel is stale only after a mesh change | steps 2.2, 2.4 |
| 3 | The layout is kept while the mesh is unchanged; a new one removes the channels not baked | steps 2.3, 2.4 |
| 4 | Albedo excludes the MSFS AO multiply | step 2.6 |
| 5 | Apply writes each channel into its target; faces without a master keep their pixels | steps 2.1, 2.8 |
| 6 | The toggles and the Object box lines | step 3 |
| 7 | The suites of step 4 pass, the AO reference values equal | their last lines |

### Do Not

- Do not change the AO bake's results, the scene rule, the combines, the C++ code or `bl_info["version"]`. Do not
  bake the received light (decision 83).
- Do not write the artist guide (`docs/user/`); the reviewing session does it once the panel is settled.
- Another session's UV packer work is uncommitted in this tree: do not edit, revert, format or stage
  `fss_blender_tools/__init__.py`, `uv_pack.py`, `uv_align.py`, `uv_align_draw.py`, `tests/test_uv_pack.py` or
  `docs/journal/20261116.native-uv-packer.md`. Do not run `git checkout`, `git stash`, `git reset` or `git add`.
- Do not commit, push or deploy. The reviewing session does that.
- Never write into the aircraft repositories (`G:\e-jets-e2`, `F:\new-fss-aircraft-ejet`), the real bake folder
  `G:\e-jets-e2\assets\visual\cockpit\COCKPIT.bake`, or their textures; never write into `F:\glider`. Download nothing.
- Never stop or touch a running `blender.exe` you did not start (the maintainer's, the other session's). Before each
  run wait until `tasklist | grep -i blender.exe` shows none of yours; run one Blender of your own at a time.
- **CPU rule (the machine crashes otherwise):** every test run goes through
  `C:\Users\uray\.claude\tools\cpucap\cpucap.exe 50 <command>`, one heavy job at a time; a C++ build (none is needed
  here) would be `C:\Users\uray\.claude\tools\cpucap\cpucap.exe 50 cmd /c native\ao_copy\build.cmd`, at most 12
  compiler threads, never `/MP` without a number, never `/affinity`, never a build and a test run at the same time.
- Background Blender only:
  `C:\Users\uray\.claude\tools\cpucap\cpucap.exe 50 G:\e-jets-e2\tools\blender\blender.exe -b --factory-startup --python tests\<suite>.py`

## Session 62: Material Channels in the Master (Decisions 79 to 83), Built

- **Date:** 20261118
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5 (a Claude sub-agent executing Session 61's hand-off)
- **Base Commit SHA:** 18d11d6 (`fss_blender_tools` 0.103.0, version not raised); the UV packer work named in Session
  61 was committed as 18d11d6 before this session started, and its files were not touched

### Built

- `ao_bake_core.py`: `CHANNEL_GROUPS` (`albedo`, `rough`, `metal`, `emis`), `CHANNEL_KINDS` (the components
  `albedo_r`, `albedo_g`, `albedo_b`, `rough`, `metal`, `emis_r`, `emis_g`, `emis_b`), `CHANNEL_SRGB`,
  `CHANNEL_SAMPLES = 16`; `channel_paths`, `srgb_encode` and `srgb_decode`; `write_layout` (the .ao.npz as
  `MasterWriter.finish` writes it); `same_layout` (decision 81: a layout built again against the stored one: size,
  islands and rectangles equal, master UVs within 1e-6 px); `ChannelWriter` (one 16-bit grey JPEG XL image of a
  master, with the .ao.map and .ao.npz written for a new layout, or the codes compared with the stored .ao.map for a
  kept one); `bake_paged_multi` (`bake_paged` for several images of one layout, each tile baked once, the composition
  without the corner rule). `all_master_files` lists the channel images (a relinked master moves them).
  `open_master(..., map_page=None)`: the map's page file can be named per image, so a channel's three components can
  be open at once. `MasterWriter`, `bake_paged`, `copy_master` and the C++ code are unchanged.
- `ao_bake_runner.py`:
  - The layout (rule item 5): `kept_layout` and `layout_for`. While the index entry's mesh hash is the item's and its
    .ao.npz (not schema 1) and .ao.map exist with the same triangles, the layout is built again at the stored density
    (the skirt is not stored) and kept when `same_layout` says it equals the stored one; otherwise a new layout at
    the density of the UVs now, said in the log when the stored one could not be built again. The AO bake
    (`bake_item`) takes its layout from `layout_for`: with the UVs unchanged the density is the same and the master
    is the one of before; the AO's files are written as before (MasterWriter). After an AO bake into a new layout the
    material channel files are removed and the entry has no `channels`; in a kept layout the entry keeps them.
  - `channel_item` (rule items 1, 2, 6): the triangles of the master (as `bake_item` selects them), the layout from
    `layout_for`, then per tile a temporary mesh of the core triangles and one of the skirt triangles (each skirt
    corner as barycentric weights of its source triangle's corners) carrying, per corner, the evaluated mesh's
    object-space positions, corner normals, material index, UV maps, colour attributes and generic attributes (point,
    corner and face domains), its texture space, the master's UV relative to the tile as the active UV map and the
    mesh's own render UV map still the render one; placed at the source object's world matrix. Per channel each slot's
    material is copied, the copy's Material Output Surface routed to an Emission shader (strength 1) fed by what
    reaches the Principled BSDF's socket (albedo: the `.Apply AO Group` node's first input when the Base Color comes
    from it), and one Cycles EMIT bake per tile part fills the tile image (pre-filled with -1, margin 0). The core
    and skirt values are combined as the AO's; albedo and emissive sRGB-encoded, roughness and metal linear (red),
    quantized to 16 bit; `bake_paged_multi` composes and writes every component. A new layout removes the AO image,
    the raw AO, the curvature, the thumbnail and every channel's images before the new files are put in place; the
    entry is then written anew with the layout's fields and `channels` only (no AO fields). The entry's
    `channels[group]` holds `mesh_hash`, `baked_at`, `samples`, `object` (the copy), `skipped` (texture sets without
    a Principled BSDF on the Material Output: they bake black, said in the log and in the job's result note) and
    `map_differs_px` (a kept layout: pixels where this bake's coverage differs from the stored map; 0 in every bake
    of the tests).
  - `run_item`: the AO (or the curvature and denoise alone), then the channels; `step` and the FSS OptiX fallback run
    it. A job item without `channels` runs as before. `FSS_AO_BAKE_CHANNEL_SAMPLES` overrides the samples (tests).
- `ao_bake_store.py`: `channel_grade` and `channel_grades` (rule item 4: `UP_TO_DATE`, `MESH`, `MISSING`; only the
  groups the entry has), written by `refresh` and `regrade` as the item's `ch_grades`; `channel_choice` (decision 80:
  Selected all chosen channels, Missing and Stale those not up to date); `bake_auto(..., groups, channels)` puts the
  channels into Bake Selected's choice the same way (a master with nothing out of date is baked in full);
  `make_job(..., channels, channel_objects)` writes the job item's `channels` and `channel_object`.
- `ao_bake_apply.py` (rule item 8): `ChannelTarget` (Target ORM Texture: the base colour, ORM or emissive slot's
  texture in place, its other channels and alpha kept, a copy in `albedo_backup`, `orm_backup` or `emis_backup` of the
  bake folder before the first write, an 8-bit file written 16-bit with its values kept; Separate File:
  `<material>.albedo.png`, `<material>.emissive.png`, roughness and metal into the green and blue of the AO's
  ORM-layout file, refused with Channels Greyscale; a new separate file white, the emissive black), `channel_problem`
  (missing, `mesh changed`, `no Principled BSDF`), `apply_set_steps(..., kind=<group>, ctx_cache)`: the AO's copy,
  padding, per-object rule and bands, no Soften; the components open at once (`_chan_open`); faces without an
  up-to-date master of the channel keep their pixels and are listed as `kept (no up-to-date master)`;
  `apply_steps` runs the ticked channels after AO and CM per texture set with one `copy_context` per master, set and
  target size. The AO's and CM's Apply paths are the same code with one component (the padding list carries a list
  of values).
- `ao_bake.py` (rule item 7): `bake_albedo`, `bake_rough`, `bake_metal`, `bake_emis`, `apply_albedo`, `apply_rough`,
  `apply_metal`, `apply_emis` (off by default); `bake_groups`, `apply_groups`, `channel_copies` (rule item 6:
  decision 77's choice from the selection, else the first copy by name). `fss.ao_bake_start` (every scope), `fss.ao_run`,
  `run_problems` and Select's Missing and Stale take the channels. The Run panel draws two rows under the AO and CM
  ticks, `Bake` and `Apply`, each with the toggles Albedo, Roughness, Metal, Emissive. The Object box draws one line
  per channel the master has, `Albedo: up to date` (`mesh changed`, `missing`), and Bake Copy for a shared mesh with
  two or more copies selected while a channel is ticked to bake (One Instance as before).
- Tests: `tests/test_ao_bake.py` gets `test_material_channels` (last in the suite, in its own scene
  `tests/_work/ao_bake_channels/channels.blend`, its Refreshes in `rule_unrecorded()`; `-- only=test_material_channels`
  runs it alone); `tests/test_panels.py` gets `test_ao_channel_toggles`.

The bake and Apply boxes of the hand-off's rule item 7 have had no ticks since decision 75 (the Run panel holds the
AO and CM ticks of the bake and of Apply, and its Run button is the only bake and apply button); the channel toggles
are therefore in the Run panel beside the AO and CM ticks, as two rows, and AO keeps its own tick there. For the
maintainer to confirm at the review.

### Measurements

Step 2.2 and the samples: T1 (a 0.2 m plane, UVs over 80 % of a 256 px set, the master 4096 px/m, 829 x 829 px)
whose base colour is an 8-bit sRGB texture of 3 x 3-box-blurred noise read through the render UV map (TexUV, the
same coordinates as the set's UVMap), Linear interpolation; Apply into a separate 256 px file in the same UVs,
compared with the source on the 124,848 face channel values:

| Bake | Mean difference | Max difference | Within 1/255 |
| :--- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Linear interpolation (the material as built) | 0.0125 (3.2/255) | 0.0632 (16.1/255) | 19.6 % |
| Closest interpolation (the same texture) | 0.0038 (1.0/255) | 0.0206 (5.3/255) | 58.6 % |

The difference is mostly the texture's bilinear interpolation in Cycles: the master holds the interpolated texture
(4 master pixels per texel), and Apply averages the master over each texture pixel, so each texel gets about an
eighth of each neighbour per axis. With Closest the rest is the master pixels that straddle texel borders.

Step 2.3: T1's UVMap scaled by 0.5 and moved (uv / 2 + 52 / 256: each texture pixel covers 2 x 2 source texels), AO
alone baked (layout kept at 4096 px/m, the UVs now give 2048), Apply into the repacked UVs, compared with the 2 x 2
box of the source on 31,212 face channel values: mean 0.0064 (1.6/255), max 0.0527 (13.4/255), 38.1 % within 1/255.

Samples (rule item 2), T1's albedo baked alone at 1, 4, 16, 64 and 256 samples (a fixed seed), against 256:

| Samples | Master max | Master 99.9 % | Master within 1/65535 | Texture max | Texture mean | Texture within 1/65535 |
| ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 1 | 747 | 278 | 35.1 % | 127 | 2.95 | 42.0 % |
| 4 | 787 | 372 | 1.8 % | 121 | 14.59 | 7.4 % |
| 16 | 368 | 104 | 7.4 % | 37 | 3.73 | 27.8 % |
| 64 | 181 | 50 | 26.9 % | 19 | 0.97 | 77.8 % |

(in 1/65535; the texture: Apply's 256 px file of each bake against the 256-sample bake's, face values.) No count up
to 64 gives values stable within 1/65535 on this texture, in the master or in the applied texture; 1 sample is the
pixel centre (no jitter), which is why it is closer than 4. The default stays 16 (`CHANNEL_SAMPLES`): the applied
texture is within 37/65535 (0.14/255) of 256 samples, and the bake of this master took 0.6 s against 4.0 s at 256.
A proposal for the maintainer (64 halves that difference and doubles the time).

### Not Verified

- Real data: no cockpit object was baked; nothing was opened in an interactive Blender (the Run rows and Object box
  lines were checked through the recording layout only).
- The MSFS material's own tree (the exporter's Principled with its groups and the emissive scale): only plain
  Principled trees built in the test, and a group named `.Apply AO Group` built there.
- FSS OptiX AO with channels baked by Cycles in the same layout: `map_differs_px` counts the pixels where the stored
  map (written by whichever bake made the layout) differs from the channel bake's coverage; 0 with Cycles in every
  test bake, not measured with FSS OptiX. Where it is not 0 the channel's image is read through the stored map.
- A layout that cannot be built again (an object scaled or rotated so the master UVs move by more than 1e-6 px):
  falls back to a new layout by the code; not tested.
- Materials that read Object Info, the object's custom properties, an Attribute node of the object or instancer
  domain, or Generated coordinates of a mesh with modifiers: the temporary bake mesh does not carry those as the
  original does. Edge-domain attributes are not carried. More than seven UV maps leave no room for the bake's own.
- Cancel during the channels of an item whose AO was baked first in the same job: the AO stays baked, the channels
  keep their old files (in a new layout they were removed with the AO's commit); the result says cancelled.
- Apply of roughness and metal into the AO's separate ORM-layout file: a later AO Apply of a fresh (not per-object)
  file writes green and blue white again, or from the ORM texture, as before this change.
- Not committed, not deployed, the artist guide not written (the hand-off's Do Not).

## Session 65: Hand-Off, the Run Grid, Apply Anyway, Single-Channel Files, Shared Emissive (Decisions 84 to 87)

- **Date:** 20261118
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5 (the hand-off; executed by the sub-agent of Session 62, which appends
  Session 66)
- **Base Commit SHA:** 18d11d6, plus Sessions 61 to 64 (uncommitted, `fss_blender_tools` 0.104.1)

### Maintainer Decisions

The maintainer's answers to Session 63's open points:

84. **The Run panel is one grid of ticks.** The panel chooses what Run does, so every channel has a Bake and an Apply
    tick in the grid AO and CM have: rows AO, CM, Albedo, Roughness, Metal, Emissive; left `<Channel> Bake Selected
    (n)`, right `Apply <Channel> to Texture (n objs, m sets)`. The rows of toggle buttons go.
85. **A channel whose mesh changed is applied anyway, with the warning,** as AO (decision 73): never refused. Faces
    without a master of the channel still keep their pixels (decision 82).
86. **Target File: roughness and metal each in their own file,** single-channel (grey) 16-bit:
    `<material>.roughness.png` and `<material>.metal.png` in the output folder, not the green and blue of the AO's
    ORM-layout file. Target in place is unchanged (the ORM texture's green and blue).
87. **Shared meshes:** albedo, roughness and metal come from one copy (decision 77's choice, as built: the copies
    share the material). Emissive is combined over the copies as AO is, by the mesh's Copies setting (Average, Most
    Lit, Darkest, One Instance; the scene's Shared Meshes default).

### The Rule

1. **The grid (decision 84):** `FSS_PT_AORun.draw`: the six rows in one `grid_flow` of two columns, the material
   channels' ticks the existing `bake_<group>` and `apply_<group>` properties drawn as checkboxes (not `toggle=True`),
   the labels as AO's with the channel's name (Albedo, Roughness, Metal, Emissive). The alert of a right cell (a
   master whose mesh changed among the selection, decision 73) per channel, from its channel grade.
2. **Apply anyway (decision 85):** a channel graded `MESH` is applied as an up-to-date one; the warning line under
   the grid counts AO and the channels (`<n> mesh changed since its bake: applied anyway`), and Apply's report lists
   them as AO's are. Only a missing channel keeps its faces' pixels.
3. **Single-channel files (decision 86):** Target File writes `<material>.roughness.png` and `<material>.metal.png`
   (16-bit grey, PNG colour type 0), created black where no face is written (as a new file's untouched pixels), per
   object as the others; the AO's ORM-layout file's green and blue are not touched by the channels any more.
4. **Shared emissive (decision 87):** for a shared mesh whose Copies is not One Instance, the emissive is baked once
   per copy (each copy's own material slots, object-linked materials included) into the same layout and combined per
   master pixel: Average the mean of the copies' colours; Most Lit and Darkest the colour of the copy whose luminance
   (Rec. 709 weights on the linear values) is the highest or lowest at that pixel, its three components together, so
   no colour is mixed from two copies (proposal: the maintainer may ask for per-component instead). One Instance as
   built (decision 77's copy). The entry's `channels['emis']` records the combine and the copies.

### Steps

1. Build items 1 to 4.
2. Tests in `tests/test_ao_bake.py` `test_material_channels`:
   1. A mesh edited after an Albedo bake, Apply Albedo: written (the master's values), reported as mesh changed.
   2. Target File with Roughness and Metal: two single-channel 16-bit files with the values; the ORM-layout file of
      an AO Apply in the same run has its green and blue as AO alone writes them.
   3. A shared mesh of three copies with object-linked materials of emissive colours whose luminances differ: Average,
      Most Lit, Darkest and One Instance each give the expected colour at a pixel (Most Lit and Darkest a copy's colour
      exactly); albedo from the chosen copy as before.
3. `tests/test_panels.py` `test_ao_channel_toggles`: the grid draws 12 checkboxes in the order of item 1, none with
   `toggle`; the alert per channel.
4. The suites: `tests/test_ao_bake.py` (whole), `tests/test_panels.py`, `tests/test_ao_bake_native_job.py`.
5. Append Session 66: what was built, each finish line MET or NOT MET with its numbers, what was not verified. Do not
   commit.

### Finish Lines

| # | Line | How it is checked |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | One grid of 12 ticks, AO to Emissive, Bake left and Apply right | step 3 |
| 2 | A mesh-changed channel is applied, with the warning | step 2.1 |
| 3 | Roughness and metal in their own single-channel 16-bit files | step 2.2 |
| 4 | Shared emissive by each of the four Copies settings | step 2.3 |
| 5 | The suites pass, the AO reference values equal | their last lines |

### Do Not

As Session 61's Do Not, all of it (the CPU rule with `cpucap.exe 50`, one Blender of your own at a time, no git
writes, no commit, deploy or version change, no writes into the aircraft repositories, the real bake folder or

## Session 66: The Run Grid, Apply Anyway, Single-Channel Files, Shared Emissive (Decisions 84 to 87), Built

- **Date:** 20261118
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5 (the sub-agent of Session 62, executing Session 65's hand-off)
- **Base Commit SHA:** 18d11d6, plus Sessions 61 to 65 (uncommitted, `fss_blender_tools` 0.104.1, version not
  raised); Session 64's Channel Samples kept as it was

### Built

- **Rule item 1, the grid (decision 84), `ao_bake.py`:** `FSS_PT_AORun.draw` draws one `grid_flow` of two columns,
  rows AO, CM, Albedo, Roughness, Metal, Emissive, each `<Name> Bake Selected (n)` left and `Apply <Name> to Texture
  (n objs, m sets)` right, the channels' existing `bake_<group>` and `apply_<group>` properties as checkboxes. The two
  rows of toggle buttons are gone. A right cell is alert while it is ticked and a selected master's grade of it is
  mesh changed (AO and CM: the AO grade, as before; a channel: its channel grade).
- **Rule item 2, apply anyway (decision 85), `ao_bake_apply.py` and `ao_bake.py`:** `channel_problem` still says
  `mesh changed`, and `apply_set_steps` now applies such a channel as an up-to-date one and lists the object in the
  report's `mesh_changed` (the report line `mesh changed since its bake, applied anyway: ...`, as AO's). Only a
  missing master, a texture set without a Principled BSDF or fewer triangles than baked keep their faces' pixels. The
  warning under the grid counts the selected masters that are mesh changed in any ticked Apply (AO, CM or a
  channel), each master once.
- **Rule item 3, single-channel files (decision 86), `ao_bake_apply.py`:** with Target Separate File, `ChannelTarget`
  writes roughness into `<material>.roughness.png` and metal into `<material>.metal.png` in the output folder, one
  channel, 16-bit (PNG colour type 0), a new file black where no face is written, per object as the others. The
  Channels setting (Greyscale or Red (ORM)) no longer matters for them, so the refusal of Session 62 is gone; the
  AO's ORM-layout file is not touched by the channels. Target ORM Texture is unchanged (the ORM texture's green and
  blue). The tooltips of the two Apply ticks name the new files.
- **Rule item 4, shared emissive (decision 87), `ao_bake_runner.py`:** for a job item with copies whose combine is
  not `ONE`, `channel_item` routes each copy's own slot materials (object-linked included) to the Emission shader and
  bakes the emissive once per copy per tile part, the temporary meshes placed at that copy's world matrix; the
  copies must have the master's evaluated mesh (a copy that has not is left out with a note).
  `combine_copies` combines the linear colours per pixel: `AVERAGE` the mean, `LIGHTEST` and `DARKEST` the
  colour of the copy whose Rec. 709 luminance is the highest or lowest there, its three components together (the
  first copy on a tie); then sRGB-encoded as before. Albedo, roughness and metal stay from the one copy (the bake's
  choice). One Instance: as built (the bake's choice of copy). The entry's `channels['emis']` gets `combine` and
  `copies` for a shared mesh. The Object box shows Bake Copy for a shared mesh only while Albedo, Roughness or Metal
  is ticked to bake (Emissive alone has no copy to choose).
- **Tests:** `tests/test_ao_bake.py` (`test_material_channels`, the scene with three copies E1 to E3 of a shared mesh
  added) and `tests/test_panels.py` (`test_ao_channel_toggles`; the recording layout also notes the alert on `prop`).

## Session 68: Hand-Off, Channels From the Vertex Colour (Decisions 88 to 90)

- **Date:** 20261118
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5 (the hand-off; executed by the sub-agent of Sessions 62 and 66, which
  appends Session 69)
- **Base Commit SHA:** 18d11d6, plus Sessions 61 to 67 (uncommitted, `fss_blender_tools` 0.105.0)

### Why

The maintainer wants to paint AO, roughness and metal (and the albedo) as a vertex colour and bake it into the master,
without a texture and without editing the material: red as an occlusion mask, green as roughness, blue as metal.

### Maintainer Decisions

88. **A vertex colour source per channel, off by default:** AO from Vertex Color Red, Roughness from Vertex Color
    Green, Metal from Vertex Color Blue, Albedo from Vertex Color (RGB). Each is a checkbox in the Run panel under the
    grid, shown only while that channel's Bake tick is on. Off: the channel bakes as before (AO traced, the others
    from the material). On: the channel is neither traced nor read from the material, only from the vertex colour.
89. **Overwrite or Multiply,** shown beside a ticked source. Overwrite: the master's channel becomes the vertex
    colour's value. Multiply: the master's current value times the vertex colour's (a red of 1.0 changes nothing);
    with no master of the channel, or a new layout (the mesh changed), it multiplies onto 1.0 (the same as Overwrite)
    and the report says so. Multiply edits the master: done twice, it multiplies twice (the tooltip says so). The AO's
    Multiply comes after its denoise; the curvature is not affected (it comes from the geometry).
90. **The source is the mesh's active colour attribute** (the highlighted row in Object Data > Color Attributes, the
    one Vertex Paint paints), not the render one (the camera icon), which the material's Color Attribute node and the
    glTF export read; so the artist keeps the real vertex colours as the render attribute and paints a second one for
    the bake. An object without a colour attribute: the channel is skipped for it and listed in the report. A shared
    mesh: the same values for every copy (the attribute is the mesh's). Emissive has no vertex colour source.

### The Rule

1. **Settings** (`FSS_AOBakeSettings`): `vc_ao`, `vc_rough`, `vc_metal`, `vc_albedo` (BoolProperty, False) and
   `vc_ao_mode`, `vc_rough_mode`, `vc_metal_mode`, `vc_albedo_mode` (EnumProperty `OVERWRITE` / `MULTIPLY`, default
   `MULTIPLY`, a proposal: with no master it gives the same as Overwrite, and with 1.0 it changes nothing). Carried in
   the job's settings.
2. **The panel** (`FSS_PT_AORun.draw`), under the grid: for each of AO, Albedo, Roughness, Metal whose Bake tick is on,
   one row: the checkbox (`<Channel> from Vertex Color Red` / `Green` / `Blue` / `from Vertex Color`) and, when ticked,
   the mode expanded (Overwrite, Multiply). No other text.
3. **The bake** of a channel with its source ticked (in the runner, through the channel path of Session 62: the
   temporary mesh of the master's triangles with the evaluated mesh's attributes): an Emission material fed by a
   Color Attribute node naming the active colour attribute (its component for AO, roughness and metal; RGB for the
   albedo), baked `EMIT` into the master's tiles and bands with the core and the skirt, then the composition.
   - Values: albedo as the Color Attribute node gives it (scene linear), sRGB-encoded as the albedo master. AO,
     roughness and metal are data: a `FLOAT_COLOR` attribute's stored value; a `BYTE_COLOR` attribute's stored byte /
     255 (as its hex shows), not sRGB-decoded (the node decodes it; encode it back). Report what a grey painted at 0.5
     in Vertex Paint gives in the master for both attribute types.
   - Multiply: read the channel's current image (the kept layout, decision 81) and multiply per master pixel, the
     albedo in linear (decode, multiply, encode); where no current value is filled, 1.0.
   - The layout: as decision 81 (kept while the mesh is unchanged; a new layout removes the channels the bake does not
     bake).
4. **The AO from the vertex colour:**
   - Overwrite: the AO image is the red; no trace, no denoise; `<stem>.ao.raw` removed; the entry gets
     `ao_source: {'vertex_color': <attribute>, 'mode': 'OVERWRITE'}`; such an AO grades without its surroundings
     (never `SURROUNDINGS`) and its denoise as up to date. A later bake with the source unticked traces it again and
     drops `ao_source`.
   - Multiply: the traced AO as it is (its denoise kept) times the red. The product of every Multiply since the last
     trace or Overwrite is kept as `<stem>.ao_vc.jxl` (16-bit, the master's layout), so a later denoise-only run (its
     settings changed) gives the denoised raw AO times that product; a trace or an Overwrite removes it. The entry's
     `ao_source` records `MULTIPLY` and the attributes. The AO's grade otherwise as before.
   - The curvature: unchanged; a Bake with CM ticked bakes it as before.
5. **Material channels from the vertex colour:** the entry's `channels[group]` gets `source: {'vertex_color':
   <attribute>, 'mode': ...}`; the grade as before (mesh change only). A later bake with the source unticked reads the
   material again and drops `source`.
6. **Not affected:** Apply (it reads the masters), the combines, the scene rule, the C++ code.

### Steps

1. Build items 1 to 5.
2. Tests in `tests/test_ao_bake.py` (`test_vertex_color_channels`, a synthetic scene; the render colour attribute a
   different colour from the active one throughout, so a read of the wrong one shows):
   1. Overwrite for each of AO (red), Roughness (green), Metal (blue), Albedo (RGB): the master equals the active
      attribute's values within 1/65535 (after the encoding rules of item 3) at the vertices of a flat-coloured face,
      for a `FLOAT_COLOR` and a `BYTE_COLOR` attribute, point and corner domain; the render attribute's values nowhere.
   2. Multiply on a traced, denoised AO with red 1.0: the AO image byte-identical to before; with red 0.5: half
      within 1/65535; twice with 0.5: a quarter.
   3. Multiply with no master: equal to Overwrite. Multiply after a mesh edit: equal to Overwrite, reported.
   4. AO Overwrite, then the surroundings changed: AO stays `UP_TO_DATE`; then a bake with the source unticked: traced
      again, `ao_source` and `.ao_vc.jxl` gone.
   5. AO Multiply, then a denoise-only run with another strength: the result equals that denoise of the raw AO times
      the kept product.
   6. Albedo Multiply on a baked material albedo: the product in linear.
   7. An object without a colour attribute: skipped and reported; its master unchanged.
   8. The material's own vertex colour (render attribute) is not in a vertex colour source bake.
3. `tests/test_panels.py`: the source rows only for ticked Bake channels, the mode only when the source is ticked; no
   Emissive row.
4. The suites: `tests/test_ao_bake.py` (whole), `tests/test_panels.py`, `tests/test_ao_bake_native_job.py`,
   `tests/test_ao_bake_native.py`.
5. Append Session 69: what was built, each finish line MET or NOT MET with its numbers, the 0.5-grey report of item 3,
   what was not verified. Do not commit.

### Finish Lines

| # | Line | How it is checked |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | Overwrite gives the active attribute's values, never the render one's | steps 2.1, 2.8 |
| 2 | Multiply: 1.0 changes nothing, it compounds, no master or a new layout is as Overwrite | steps 2.2, 2.3 |
| 3 | The vertex colour AO ignores the surroundings; an unticked bake traces again | step 2.4 |
| 4 | A denoise-only run keeps the Multiply | step 2.5 |
| 5 | The panel rows as decision 88 | step 3 |
| 6 | The suites pass, the AO reference values equal | their last lines |

### Do Not

As Session 61's Do Not, all of it: every Blender run through `C:\Users\uray\.claude\tools\cpucap\cpucap.exe 50`, one
Blender of your own at a time, never a `blender.exe` you did not start; no C++ build (if one ever were needed, only
`cpucap.exe 50 cmd /c native\ao_copy\build.cmd`, at most 12 compiler threads, never `/MP` without a number, never
`/affinity`, never with a test run); no git writes, no commit, deploy or version change; no writes into the aircraft
repositories, the real bake folder or `F:\glider`; no Substance in any text; no artist guide. No Emissive source.

### Next

Session 69: decisions 88 to 90 as above; then the review.

## Session 69: Channels From the Vertex Colour (Decisions 88 to 90), Built

- **Date:** 20261118
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5 (the sub-agent of Sessions 62 and 66, executing Session 68's hand-off)
- **Base Commit SHA:** 18d11d6, plus Sessions 61 to 68 (uncommitted, `fss_blender_tools` 0.105.0, version not raised)

### Built

- **Rule item 1, settings (`ao_bake.py`):** `vc_ao`, `vc_albedo`, `vc_rough`, `vc_metal` (off; their names are the
  panel text: `AO from Vertex Color Red`, `Albedo from Vertex Color`, `Roughness from Vertex Color Green`, `Metal from
  Vertex Color Blue`) and `vc_<group>_mode` (`OVERWRITE` / `MULTIPLY`, default `MULTIPLY`; the Multiply tooltip says
  that each bake multiplies again and that without a master it is the same as Overwrite). The job's settings carry
  `vertex_color`, `{group: mode}` of the ticked sources (`store.vertex_color_sources`); the runner uses one only for a
  channel it bakes.
- **Rule item 2, the panel (`FSS_PT_AORun.draw`):** under the grid, for AO, Albedo, Roughness and Metal in that order,
  one row per ticked Bake: the source checkbox and, while it is ticked, the mode expanded. No Emissive row, no other
  text.
- **Rule item 3, the bake (`ao_bake_runner.py`):** `channel_item(item, coll, ao=False)` takes the AO as one more
  group (its image the master's own `.ao.jxl`). A group whose source is ticked gets, on every slot, one material:
  an Emission shader fed by a Color Attribute node of the mesh's active colour attribute (`active_color`; Separate
  Color's red, green or blue for the AO, roughness and metal, the colour for the albedo), baked `EMIT` per tile with
  the core and the skirt and composed as the other channels. Values: the albedo sRGB-encoded from the node's scene
  linear value; the AO, roughness and metal clipped as they are from a `FLOAT_COLOR` attribute, sRGB-encoded back
  from a `BYTE_COLOR` one (so the stored byte / 255). Multiply (decision 89): in the kept layout, each written row
  times the current image's row (the albedo decoded, multiplied, encoded), pixels this bake does not fill keep their
  old value; without a current image of the group, or in a new layout, the value as Overwrite, said in the log and
  the job's result note (`<source>: Multiply with no master of it, as Overwrite`, `... with a new layout (the mesh
  changed), as Overwrite`). An object without a colour attribute: its source groups skipped, in the log and the note
  (`Albedo from Vertex Color skipped: VX has no colour attribute`), the others baked; nothing else left: the item is
  skipped and its master unchanged. `run_item`: a job item with its AO from the vertex colour goes through
  `channel_item(ao=True)` instead of the trace, then the curvature when CM is ticked (`curvature_item`, as before).
- **Rule item 4, the AO:**
  - Overwrite: the AO image is the red; `.ao.raw.jxl` and `.ao_vc.jxl` removed; the entry gets `ao_source:
    {vertex_color, mode: 'OVERWRITE', attributes}` and `denoise: None`. `store.grade` skips the surroundings for it and
    `store.dn_grade` gives `UP_TO_DATE` (`vc_overwritten`). A trace (`bake_item`) writes the entry anew, so
    `ao_source` goes, and removes `.ao_vc.jxl`.
  - Multiply on a traced AO: the AO times the red, its denoise kept; the product of every Multiply since the last trace
    or Overwrite written as `<stem>.ao_vc.jxl` (the previous product times this red); `ao_source` `MULTIPLY` with the
    attributes in order. A denoise-only run (`curvature_item`) multiplies the denoised (or, Denoise off, restored)
    raw bake by the product again (`multiply_product`).
  - The curvature: untouched; with CM ticked it is baked as before after the AO.
- **Rule item 5, material channels:** `channels[group]['source'] = {vertex_color, mode}`; a bake from the material
  writes the group's entry anew without it. Grades as before.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_ao_bake.py` gets `test_vertex_color_channels` (last in the suite, its own scene
  `tests/_work/ao_bake_vc/vc.blend`, its Refreshes in `rule_unrecorded()`; `-- only=test_vertex_color_channels`);
  `tests/test_panels.py` gets `test_ao_vertex_color_rows`.

### Built Differently, for the Maintainer

1. **The raw bake of a never-denoised AO.** A traced AO without Denoise has no `.ao.raw.jxl`; Multiply copies the
   traced AO there before its first multiply, so a later denoise (or Denoise turned off) starts from the traced bake,
   not from the multiplied one. Without it the product would be applied twice.
2. **Multiply onto an Overwrite AO** stays an Overwrite (`mode` `OVERWRITE`, the attributes listed, no product): there
   is no traced AO under it. Multiply without a master or in a new layout records `OVERWRITE` too.
3. **Not paged:** Multiply reads the current image of each multiplied component whole (2 bytes per master pixel each,
   plus the product), and `multiply_product` the master and the product; a very large master needs that memory.
4. **The 0.5 grey (rule item 3's report).** Vertex Paint at a brush of 0.5 (the colour picker's value; the brush's
   colour property is scene linear) stores 0.5 in a `FLOAT_COLOR` attribute and the byte 188 (sRGB-encoded: its hex
   BCBCBC, `color` 0.5029) in a `BYTE_COLOR` one. In the master:

   | Attribute | Stored | AO, roughness, metal | Albedo |
   | :--- | :--- | ---: | ---: |
   | `FLOAT_COLOR` | 0.5 | 32768 (0.500) | 48192 (sRGB of 0.5) |
   | `BYTE_COLOR` | byte 188 | 48316 (0.737) | 48316 (sRGB of 0.503) |

   The same grey painted gives an AO of 0.50 in a float attribute and 0.74 in a byte one. That is rule item 3 as
   written (the byte / 255, as its hex shows). If the artist should get what the picker shows in both, the byte
   attribute's data would be read as its linear value (0.503); a proposal, not built.
5. **Most meshes have a colour attribute.** The MSFS exporter's `new_object_handler` (`msfs_handlers.py`) gives every
   new mesh a white `Color` (`BYTE_COLOR`, corner) once, unless a scene targets MSFS 2020; it is active and render. On
   such a mesh a source bake reads that white (Overwrite: AO, roughness, metal 1.0; Multiply: nothing changes) until
   the artist paints or adds another attribute. "No colour attribute" (step 2.7) happens only when it was removed.
6. **Which attribute the export writes.** Blender's glTF exporter reads the render attribute (`render_color_index`,
   `primitive_extract.py`), as decision 90 says. But the merge's preparation (`multi_export._prepare_for_merge`)
   renames the active attribute (`mesh.attributes.active_color`) to the default name, and the handler's comment says
   MSFS 2020 assets are exported with the active attribute. A merged object, or an MSFS 2020 export, may then carry
   the painted bake attribute as its vertex colour. Read in the code only; not exported.

  'vc_metal']; ...)`, `ALL TESTS PASSED`

## Session 70: Hand-Off, the Second Colour Attribute and Linear Values (Decisions 91 and 92)

- **Date:** 20261118
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5 (the hand-off; executed by the sub-agent of Sessions 62 to 69, which
  appends Session 71)
- **Base Commit SHA:** 18d11d6, plus Sessions 61 to 69 (uncommitted, `fss_blender_tools` 0.105.0; Session 69 not
  deployed)

### Why

Session 69 found two problems with decision 90's source, the mesh's active colour attribute: the artist has to make
the bake attribute active on every object, and the merge (`multi_export._prepare_for_merge`) and the MSFS 2020 export
write the active attribute, so a bake attribute left active ships as the model's vertex colour. It also found that a
grey painted at 0.5 gives 0.50 from a float attribute but 0.74 from a byte one (Session 68 item 3's byte / 255 rule,
the hand-off's, not the maintainer's). The maintainer asked for an option that bakes from the second colour attribute
without the artist activating it.

### Maintainer Decisions

91. **The vertex colour sources read the Second Color Attribute by default:** the second row of the mesh's Color
    Attributes list, whatever is active or render; the option's other choice is Active Attribute (decision 90's). The
    artist leaves the active and render attributes on the real vertex colours. Nothing in the artist's file is
    switched: the bake reads the attribute by name in its snapshot. An object with fewer than two colour attributes
    (Second; none for Active) is skipped for those channels and listed in the report, so the white `Color` attribute
    the MSFS add-on gives a new mesh is never read as a source.
92. **The values are linear for both attribute types:** a grey painted at 0.5 gives 0.5 in AO, roughness and metal,
    from a float or a byte attribute (Blender's colour picker values are linear). The albedo is read the same way and
    stored sRGB-encoded as before. This replaces Session 68 item 3's byte / 255 rule.

### The Rule

1. **Setting:** `vc_attribute` (EnumProperty `SECOND` / `ACTIVE`, names "Second Color Attribute" and "Active
   Attribute", default `SECOND`), carried in the job's settings. Drawn once, under the source rows of decision 88,
   while any source is ticked.
2. **The runner:** the source attribute is `mesh.color_attributes[1]` of the original mesh (its name, then found on
   the evaluated mesh by name) for `SECOND`, the active colour attribute for `ACTIVE`; the Color Attribute node names
   it. The value is the node's output for every attribute type (linear); the byte re-encoding of Session 69 goes.
3. **The entry:** `ao_source` and `channels[group]['source']` record the attribute's name and `SECOND` or `ACTIVE`.
4. **Skips:** as decision 91; the report says `<object>: no second colour attribute` (or `no colour attribute`).

### Steps

1. Build items 1 to 4.
2. Tests in `tests/test_ao_bake.py` `test_vertex_color_channels`, changed for the new default; each case's mesh has a
   first attribute (active and render, its own colour) and a second one painted:
   1. `SECOND`: every Overwrite equals the second attribute's values (deviation 0), never the first's; the first
      attribute stays active and render after the bake, the .blend's attributes unchanged.
   2. `ACTIVE` with the second attribute active: its values; with the first active: the first's.
   3. A grey 0.5 painted (Set Vertex Colors) in a float and in a byte attribute: both give 32768 within 1/65535 (1
      byte step allowed: report the value) in AO, roughness and metal.
   4. An object with one colour attribute under `SECOND`: skipped, reported, its master unchanged.
   5. The other cases of Session 69 still pass under the new default.
3. `tests/test_panels.py`: the `vc_attribute` row only while a source is ticked.
4. The suites: `tests/test_ao_bake.py` (whole), `tests/test_panels.py`, `tests/test_ao_bake_native_job.py`,
   `tests/test_ao_bake_native.py`.
5. Append Session 71: what was built, each finish line MET or NOT MET with its numbers, what was not verified. Do not
   commit.

### Finish Lines

| # | Line | How it is checked |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | The second attribute is read without being activated; the file's attributes unchanged | step 2.1 |
| 2 | Active Attribute reads the active one | step 2.2 |
| 3 | A painted 0.5 gives 0.5 from a float and a byte attribute | step 2.3 |
| 4 | One attribute only: skipped and reported | step 2.4 |
| 5 | The suites pass, the AO reference values equal | their last lines |

### Do Not

As Session 61's Do Not, all of it: every Blender run through `C:\Users\uray\.claude\tools\cpucap\cpucap.exe 50`, one
Blender of your own at a time, never a `blender.exe` you did not start; no C++ build (if one ever were needed, only
`cpucap.exe 50 cmd /c native\ao_copy\build.cmd`, at most 12 compiler threads, never `/MP` without a number, never
`/affinity`, never with a test run); no git writes, no commit, deploy or version change; no writes into the aircraft
repositories, the real bake folder or `F:\glider`; no Substance in any text; no artist guide.

Session 71: decisions 91 and 92 as above; then the review.

## Session 73: Hand-Off, Fetch to Vertex Colour and the Emissive Source (Decisions 93 to 95)

- **Date:** 20261118
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5 (the hand-off; executed by the sub-agent of Sessions 62 to 71, which
  appends Session 74)
- **Base Commit SHA:** 18d11d6, plus Sessions 61 to 72 (uncommitted, `fss_blender_tools` 0.106.0)

### Why

The maintainer wants to edit a master's values as vertex colour: fetch them from the master into the colour attribute,
paint over them, and bake them back with the vertex colour source.

### Maintainer Decisions

93. **Three buttons, Fetch Albedo, Fetch ORM and Fetch Emis,** in the Run panel: for the selected objects, the master
    read at every face corner into the attribute the Read option names (decision 91: the second colour attribute by
    default). Fetch ORM: red the AO, green the roughness, blue the metal; a channel without a master keeps that
    component as it is. Fetch Albedo and Fetch Emis: the colour. The three share the one attribute, so a Fetch
    replaces what was painted there. An operator in the artist's Blender, undone with Ctrl+Z.
94. **Corner values, and the limits:** one value per face corner (a vertex on two faces can have two). An object whose
    modifiers change the mesh (its evaluated corners are not the original's: Mirror, Subdivision, Bevel) is skipped
    and reported. A mesh with only its first colour attribute gets a second one added (Second Color Attribute); a
    mesh with none is skipped, so a new first attribute (the exported one) is never made. A master from a vertex
    colour comes back as it was painted; a master with detail between the vertices keeps only its corner values, so
    baking it back with Overwrite smooths it (Multiply with a mask keeps the detail).
95. **Emissive from Vertex Color,** with Overwrite and Multiply, as the other channels' sources (decisions 88, 89):
    RGB, read as the albedo is (linear, stored sRGB-encoded). A shared mesh's colour is the mesh's, the same for every
    copy, so its Copies setting does not change a vertex colour emissive.

### The Rule

1. **The Emissive source:** `vc_emis` and `vc_emis_mode` as the others; its row under the grid while Bake Emissive is
   ticked; the runner's source path for the group `emis`; the entry's `channels['emis']['source']`.
2. **The operator** `fss.ao_fetch_vertex_color` (`what`: `ALBEDO`, `ORM`, `EMIS`), drawn as one row of three buttons
   under the source rows of the Run panel, always; disabled (with the reason, as Run's problems are) without a Refresh
   or without a selected object that has a master of the asked channels.
   - Per selected mesh object, per texture set with a master: the master's layout (`.ao.npz`), the triangles' master
     UVs, `tri_src`, the evaluated triangles' `loops`. When the evaluated mesh's loop count or loop-to-vertex map
     differs from the original mesh's: skipped, reported (`<object>: its modifiers change the mesh`).
   - Each master triangle's three corners: the channel's value read at the corner's master position, clamped onto the
     triangle's own baked texels (as `copy_master` reads inside a face: never a neighbour's island), bilinear; the
     values decoded as Apply decodes them (albedo and emissive from sRGB to linear). A corner shared by several
     triangles of one polygon takes the mean.
   - Written into the target attribute (Read: the second, else the active one) per corner. When that attribute is in
     the point domain or a byte type: converted to a corner-domain float colour attribute of the same name and
     position, its other values kept (report it). Fetch ORM writes only the components whose channel has a master;
     Fetch Albedo and Fetch Emis write RGB; alpha 1. Corners of faces without a master keep their values.
   - A mesh with one colour attribute (Second): a second one added, `FSS_Bake` (corner, float colour, white), after
     it, not active, not render. A mesh with none: skipped, reported.
   - A shared mesh: written once.
   - The report: per object the corners written and the channels, the skips with their reasons.
3. **Not affected:** the bake, Apply, the masters (Fetch only reads them).

### Steps

1. Build items 1 to 3.
2. Tests in `tests/test_ao_bake.py` (`test_vertex_color_fetch`, a synthetic scene):
   1. Round trip: a mesh with a second attribute painted (per-corner random values), bake AO, roughness, metal from it
      (Overwrite), Fetch ORM into a cleared attribute: each corner within one 8-bit step (1/255) of the painted value;
      report the largest difference. The same for Albedo and for Emissive (Fetch Albedo, Fetch Emis).
   2. A traced AO: Fetch ORM writes red, green and blue keep their values where roughness and metal have no master.
   3. A mesh with one colour attribute: `FSS_Bake` added after it, the first still active and render; a mesh with
      none: skipped, reported, no attribute added.
   4. A Mirror modifier: skipped, reported, its attributes unchanged.
   5. Undo: after `bpy.ops.ed.undo()` the attribute's values are as before the Fetch (when undo works in the
      background Blender; else say so).
   6. Emissive from Vertex Color: Overwrite and Multiply as the other channels (the cases of Session 69 for emissive).
3. `tests/test_panels.py`: the three buttons in the Run panel; the Emissive source row while Bake Emissive is ticked.
4. The suites: `tests/test_ao_bake.py` (whole), `tests/test_panels.py`, `tests/test_ao_bake_native_job.py`,
   `tests/test_ao_bake_native.py`.
5. Append Session 74: what was built, each finish line MET or NOT MET with its numbers, what was not verified. Do not
   commit.

### Finish Lines

| # | Line | How it is checked |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | A vertex colour master fetched comes back within one 8-bit step at every corner | step 2.1 |
| 2 | Fetch ORM writes only the channels with a master | step 2.2 |
| 3 | A second attribute is added only beside a first; never a first | step 2.3 |
| 4 | Modifiers that change the mesh: skipped | step 2.4 |
| 5 | Emissive from Vertex Color as the others | step 2.6 |
| 6 | The buttons and the Emissive row | step 3 |
| 7 | The suites pass, the AO reference values equal | their last lines |

As Session 61's Do Not, all of it: every Blender run through `C:\Users\uray\.claude\tools\cpucap\cpucap.exe 50`, one
Blender of your own at a time, never a `blender.exe` you did not start; no C++ build (if one ever were needed, only
`cpucap.exe 50 cmd /c native\ao_copy\build.cmd`, at most 12 compiler threads, never `/MP` without a number, never
`/affinity`, never with a test run); no git writes, no commit, deploy or version change; no writes into the aircraft
repositories, the real bake folder or `F:\glider`; no Substance in any text; no artist guide.

## Session 74: Fetch to Vertex Colour and the Emissive Source (Decisions 93 to 95), Built

- **Date:** 20261118
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5 (the sub-agent of Sessions 62 to 71, executing Session 73's hand-off)
- **Base Commit SHA:** 18d11d6, plus Sessions 61 to 73 (uncommitted, `fss_blender_tools` 0.106.0, version not raised)

### Built

- **Rule item 1, the Emissive source:** `vc_emis` ("Emissive from Vertex Color") and `vc_emis_mode` (`ao_bake.py`),
  its row under the grid while Bake Emissive is ticked (after Metal); `store.VC_GROUPS` takes `emis`; in the runner
  `emis` is an RGB source (`VC_COMP`, `VC_NAMES`), sRGB-encoded as the albedo, its Multiply in linear as the albedo's;
  a shared mesh's vertex colour emissive is baked once from the mesh's attribute, not over the copies (decision 87's
  combine skipped, no `combine` or `copies` in its entry); `channels['emis']['source']` as the others.
- **Rule item 2, the operator** `fss.ao_fetch_vertex_color` (`what` `ALBEDO`, `ORM`, `EMIS`; `ao_bake.py`, Undo
  registered), its logic `fetch_vertex_color` in `ao_bake_apply.py` (a section of its own: Fetch only reads the
  masters):
  - Drawn as one row of three buttons (Fetch Albedo, Fetch ORM, Fetch Emis) under the source rows of the Run panel,
    always; a button greyed when `fetch_problems` gives a reason, and the reasons in one line under the row (`Fetch:
    press Refresh first`, `Fetch: select the objects`, `Fetch: no Albedo master in the selection`). The last Fetch's
    report lines are drawn under it.
  - Per selected mesh object with a master of the asked groups (ORM: the AO, roughness and metal, each only when its
    master is there; Albedo; Emis): skipped when the evaluated mesh's loop count or loop-to-vertex map is not the
    original's (`<object>: its modifiers change the mesh`), and a group whose master is graded mesh changed is
    skipped and said (my choice: a master of another mesh read at these corners would be wrong).
  - `corner_values`: per master triangle and corner, the value at the corner's master position, bilinear over the
    four nearest texels that are the triangle's own (its face in the `.ao.map`, filled), renormalized; with none of
    the four, the nearest own texel within 4 px (the report counts these). Albedo and emissive decoded from sRGB to
    linear. A loop of several triangles of one polygon gets the mean. The master images are read whole (not paged).
  - The target: Read's attribute (decision 91). Second Color Attribute: the mesh's second; beside an only one,
    `FSS_Bake` added (corner, float, white), not active, not render; with none, skipped (`<object>: no colour
    attribute`). Active Attribute: the active one, else skipped. A point-domain or byte target converted to a corner
    float attribute of the same name by Blender's Convert Color Attribute (made active for the call; the active and
    render ones put back), reported.
  - Written: the groups' components (ORM: red, green, blue each only from its master), alpha 1, only at corners of
    faces a master covers; the rest kept. A shared mesh is written once (the other copies reported).
- **Rule item 3:** the bake, Apply and the masters unchanged by Fetch.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_ao_bake.py` `test_vertex_color_fetch` (last in the suite, its own scene
  `tests/_work/ao_bake_fetch/fetch.blend`, `-- only=test_vertex_color_fetch`); `tests/test_panels.py`
  `test_ao_fetch_buttons`, `test_ao_vertex_color_rows` with the Emissive row, and `fss.ao_fetch_vertex_color` among
  the AO Bake operators `test_ao_bake_unavailable` checks.

## Session 76: Hand-Off, Albedo and Emissive Targets in Texture Target (Decisions 96 and 97)

- **Date:** 20261119
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5 (the hand-off; executed by a sub-agent, which appends Session 77)
- **Base Commit SHA:** 53b4947 (`fss_blender_tools` 0.107.0)

### Why

The maintainer ticked Apply Albedo to Texture and found no place in Texture Target saying where it goes: the
material channels follow the AO section's Target (ORM Texture: the base colour and emissive slots' textures in place;
Separate File: `<material>.albedo.png` and `<material>.emissive.png` in the AO's output folder at its resolution), and
the problem line under the box checks only the ORM slot, so a missing base colour texture shows only as a failed set
during Apply.

### Maintainer Decisions

96. **Texture Target gets an Albedo and an Emissive section, as AO and Curvature Map have,** each shown only while its
    Apply tick in Run is on. Each has its own **Target**: in place (the base colour or emissive slot's texture,
    named, with the 8-bit note) or **Separate File**, and with Separate File its **Output Folder** (empty: the AO's),
    **File** (default `<material>.albedo.png`, `<material>.emissive.png`), **Resolution** and **Padding**, as
    Curvature Map's. Roughness and Metal stay with the AO section's Target (the ORM texture's green and blue, or their
    own files in the AO's folder).
97. **The problem line checks every ticked Apply channel's target** (a missing slot texture, an output folder that
    cannot be made) before Run, as it does for the AO.

### The Rule

1. **Settings,** following the curvature's (`curv_output_folder` with its getter, `curv_padding`, the per-set
   `curv_file_name` and `curv_resolution`): per texture set `albedo_target` and `emis_target` (EnumProperty: `SLOT`
   "Base Color Texture" / "Emissive Texture", and `FILE` "Separate File"; default `FILE`, a proposal: in place
   overwrites the artist's texture), `albedo_file_name`, `emis_file_name`, `albedo_resolution`, `emis_resolution`;
   scene `albedo_output_folder`, `emis_output_folder` (empty: the AO's), `albedo_padding`, `emis_padding` (default 8).
2. **The panel** (`_draw_target`): after the AO section and before Curvature Map, an **Albedo** section while
   `apply_albedo` is on and an **Emissive** section while `apply_emis` is on, laid out as the AO's (Target expanded;
   in place: the slot's texture name and `8-bit file: written as 16-bit`; Separate File: Output Folder, File,
   Resolution) and Padding. The AO section's heading and Target stay; its tooltip says it covers AO, Roughness and
   Metal.
3. **Apply** (`ao_bake_apply.py`): albedo and emissive written per their own Target, folder, file, resolution and
   padding; roughness and metal as before (the AO's Target). An existing separate file keeps its size, as the AO's.
4. **The problem line** (`target_problem`, and `run_problems`): per ticked Apply channel of the set, its own target's
   problem (`No texture in the base colour slot`, `No texture in the emissive slot`, the output folder); Run is
   disabled while one is there, as for the AO.
5. **The artist guide** (`docs/user/bake.md`): Texture Target's Albedo and Emissive sections and the Where Apply
   Writes table follow the change (the guide describes what exists, in the same commit).

### Steps

1. Build items 1 to 5.
2. Tests in `tests/test_ao_bake.py` (extend `test_material_channels`): Albedo in place with the AO in a separate file,
   and the other way round; Emissive into a separate file in its own folder at its own resolution and padding; a
   set without a base colour texture with Apply Albedo ticked: the problem line names it and Run refuses; the AO's
   files and the ORM texture unchanged by an Albedo-only Apply.
3. `tests/test_panels.py`: the Albedo and Emissive sections only while their Apply tick is on, their rows as item 2.
4. The suites: `tests/test_ao_bake.py` (whole), `tests/test_panels.py`, `tests/test_ao_bake_native_job.py`.
5. Append Session 77: what was built, each finish line MET or NOT MET with its numbers, what was not verified. Do not
   commit.

### Finish Lines

| # | Line | How it is checked |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | Albedo and Emissive go where their own Target says, independent of the AO's | step 2 |
| 2 | A missing slot texture of a ticked channel stops Run with its line | step 2 |
| 3 | The sections only while their Apply tick is on | step 3 |
| 4 | The suites pass, the AO reference values equal | their last lines |

### Do Not

- Do not change the bake, the masters, the other channels' targets, the C++ code or `bl_info["version"]`.
- No git writes (no add, commit, checkout, stash, reset), no commit, push or deploy.
- Never write into the aircraft repositories (`G:\e-jets-e2`, `F:\new-fss-aircraft-ejet`), the real bake folder
  `G:\e-jets-e2\assets\visual\cockpit\COCKPIT.bake`, their textures, or `F:\glider`. Download nothing.
- Never stop or touch a running `blender.exe` you did not start; one Blender of your own at a time.
- **CPU rule (the machine crashes otherwise):** every test run goes through
  `C:\Users\uray\.claude\tools\cpucap\cpucap.exe 50 <command>`, one heavy job at a time; a C++ build (none is needed
  here) would be `C:\Users\uray\.claude\tools\cpucap\cpucap.exe 50 cmd /c native\ao_copy\build.cmd`, at most 12
  compiler threads, never `/MP` without a number, never `/affinity`, never a build and a test run at the same time.
- Background Blender only:
  `C:\Users\uray\.claude\tools\cpucap\cpucap.exe 50 G:\e-jets-e2\tools\blender\blender.exe -b --factory-startup --python tests\<suite>.py`.
  From Git Bash write `cmd //c`.
- Never mention Substance in panel text, tooltips, reports or the guide.

## Session 78: Custom Roughness and Metal for a New ORM-Layout File (Decision 98)

- **Date:** 20261122
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5
- **Base Commit SHA:** 440d7f4; `fss_blender_tools` raised to **0.109.0**

### Maintainer Decision

98. **Green and Blue gets a third choice, Custom,** beside White and From ORM Texture: it shows **Roughness**
    (default 1.0) and **Metal** (default 0.0) sliders, and a new ORM-layout file (Texture Target, AO, Separate File,
    Channels Red (ORM)) is created with them in its green and blue. MSFS multiplies the material's roughness and
    metallic factors by these channels, so White (1.0, 1.0) keeps both factors and Custom 1.0 / 0.0 keeps the
    roughness factor and makes the set not metallic. An existing file keeps its green and blue, as before.

### Built

- `ao_bake.py`: `green_blue` gains `CUSTOM`; per texture set `orm_roughness` (1.0) and `orm_metal` (0.0), drawn as
  sliders while Custom is chosen.
- `ao_bake_apply.py`: `ApplyTarget.green_blue` returns the two values for Custom, written into every pixel's green
  and blue of a new file (no array of the file's size).
- `docs/user/bake.md`: the Green and Blue line.
- `tests/test_ao_bake.py` `test_apply_separate`: Custom 0.25 / 0.75 gives green 16384 and blue 49151 at every pixel
  and the same red as White; Custom 1.0 / 0.0 gives 65535 and 0.

### Re-Run

Under `cpucap.exe 50`, one at a time (logs in `tests/_work/gb_custom/`):

- `test_ao_bake`: ALL AO BAKE TESTS PASSED (346.0 s), 47 PASS and 0 FAIL, `test_apply_separate` with the Custom
  checks; the 160 reference cases and the 196 scene-rule Refreshes equal.
- `test_panels`: ALL TESTS PASSED (15 PASS).

## Session 79: Custom Roughness and Metal per Object in the Bake (Decision 99)

- **Date:** 20261122
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5
- **Base Commit SHA:** 440d7f4 (with Session 78 uncommitted); `fss_blender_tools` raised to **0.110.0**

### Maintainer Decision

99. **Roughness and Metal can be baked from a Custom value stored per object,** beside Material and Vertex Color:
    it acts like baking from the vertex colour's green and blue, without painting the value into the vertex colour.
    Each object keeps its own Roughness and Metal (its "default"), saved with the object and unchanged when its
    material changes, and reused at every bake with Custom. Overwrite and Multiply as for a vertex colour source,
    **Overwrite by default**. The maintainer chose the object over the texture set's Green and Blue Custom sliders
    (decision 98), which stay a separate setting for a new ORM file.

### Built

- `ao_bake.py`: `rough_source` and `metal_source` (Material, Vertex Color Green / Blue, Custom; one choice, read and
  written through the `vc_<g>` booleans and new `cv_<g>` booleans, so a file's ticked vertex colour source carries
  over as Vertex Color); `cv_rough_mode` and `cv_metal_mode` (Overwrite); per object `rough_value` (1.0) and
  `metal_value` (0.0). The Run panel draws the drop-down in the Roughness and Metal rows, the mode beside a source
  that is not Material, and with Custom the active object's values, a line when the selected meshes' values differ,
  or "make a mesh active" without one.
- `ao_bake_store.py`: `settings['custom_value']` ({group: mode}); a vertex colour source is not sent while the same
  channel is Custom; each job item with channels carries `custom_values` of the object its channels come from (a
  shared mesh's Copy choice).
- `ao_bake_runner.py`: `value_material` (an Emission shader of the value) on every slot of a Custom channel; Multiply,
  Multiply without a master as Overwrite (reported) and the kept layout as for a vertex colour source; the entry's
  channel `source` is `{custom, mode}`, the result's `custom_value` the values and modes.
- `docs/user/bake.md`: the Roughness and Metal drop-down in the vertex colour table, a section Custom Roughness and
  Metal. `fss_blender_tools/README.md`, `tests/README.md`, `AGENTS.md`: the decision.
- `tests/test_panels.py`: `test_ao_vertex_color_rows` with the drop-downs in the Roughness and Metal rows;
  `test_ao_custom_value_rows` (the choice, its storage and carry-over, the drawn rows and values, the differ line,
  the job settings and values).
- `tests/test_ao_bake.py` `test_custom_value_channels` (in the vertex colour scene): two objects with their own
  values in one Overwrite bake, Multiply 0.3 x 0.5, Multiply without a master as Overwrite with its note, the
  material again dropping the source.

### Not Verified

- Blender's right-click Copy to Selected on `rough_value` and `metal_value` (the guide names it): it needs the UI,
  not checked in a background Blender.
- The panel in a live Blender.

### Re-Run

Under `cpucap.exe 50`, one at a time (logs in `tests/_work/cv/`):

- `test_ao_bake`: ALL AO BAKE TESTS PASSED (373.9 s), 48 PASS and 0 FAIL; `test_custom_value_channels` (13.8 s):
  Overwrite within 1 of 65535 for both objects' own values, Multiply 0.3 x 0.5 within 1; `test_vertex_color_channels`
  unchanged; the 160 reference cases and the 196 scene-rule Refreshes equal.
- `test_panels`: ALL TESTS PASSED (16 PASS), with `test_ao_vertex_color_rows` and `test_ao_custom_value_rows`.
