## Session 81: Custom Values Written Straight Into the Master (Decision 101)

- **Date:** 20261123
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5
- **Base Commit SHA:** 440d7f4 (with Sessions 78 to 80 uncommitted); `fss_blender_tools` raised to **0.112.0**

### Why

The maintainer asked why a Custom albedo bake takes about as long as the AO. The real bake's log on
`@MERGE_COCKPIT_SHELL_FLOOR` (20494 x 24049 px, 132 tiles of 2048 px): 130 s for one grey albedo (Cycles 85 s, the
composition and the three images 28.5 s), 198 s for its AO by Cycles. Cycles ran twice per tile (the core and the
skirt), 264 runs, each with its setup, at 64 samples of one constant.

### Maintainer Decision

101. **A Custom value or colour is written straight into the master, without Cycles,** with the same result; the
     composition, Overwrite and Multiply, the images and the index as before. A bake mixing Custom and other sources
     bakes only the others with Cycles. Vertex Color sources stay on Cycles (a separate decision if wanted).

### Built

- `ao_bake_runner.py` `channel_item`: the Custom groups (`dv`) get no material and no Cycles bake; Cycles' tile
  image and meshes are made only when another group needs them.
  - **A kept layout:** the value on exactly the stored map's filled pixels, 0 elsewhere, written with the stored codes
    (`_MapRows` reads the map in the composition's row order); the map is left as it is.
  - **A new layout:** the pixels Cycles fills for another group of the same tile, else the core's and the skirt's by
    `core.raster_window` (within the island rectangles where the core is not), composed as a baked channel; the map is
    written from them.
  - The log says "no Cycles" and "written directly"; the result has `direct_s`, and baker `Custom value` when no group
    needed Cycles; the index's channel `samples` is 0. `FSS_AO_BAKE_CUSTOM_CYCLES` (tests) bakes a Custom group
    through `value_material` and Cycles as before.
- `tests/test_ao_bake.py` `test_custom_value_channels`: the four Custom channels of VM and VX baked through Cycles (the
  switch), then written directly, every pixel of the eight images compared; VM's direct albedo non-zero on exactly the
  map's filled pixels; a bake of a Custom albedo with the roughness from the material (Cycles for the roughness only).
- `docs/user/bake.md` (Channel Samples, Custom Values and Colours), `fss_blender_tools/README.md`, `AGENTS.md`.

### Two Wrong Turns, Found on the Cockpit

The synthetic scene passed all three versions; only the floor master showed the faults.

1. **The raster's pixels for a kept layout:** 81,161 of the map's filled pixels stayed 0 (Apply would read black),
   pixels Cycles' rasterizing fills and the C++ raster does not (the sampled ones lie on long shallow lines, thin
   faces), which the composition did not fill either; 4,755 pixels the other way.
2. **The map's filled pixels as baked ones:** no hole, but 2,303,430 extra pixels within 8 px of the filled ones. The
   map's filled bit is the baked pixels and the margin the composition grew from them (FILL steps inside each island's
   rectangle); fed back as baked, the composition grew the margin a second time.

The kept layout's rule above (the value on the stored filled pixels, the stored codes) is the third version.

### Not Verified

- **A new layout's coverage:** the C++ raster, not Cycles' rule, so a thin face Cycles would fill can lack filled
  pixels in a new Custom-only master (on the floor's layout the raster missed 81,161 of Cycles' 129,807,992 filled
  pixels, 0.06 %). The map is
  written from the same pixels, so Apply does not read a hole as a value; what Apply shows on such a face was not
  checked. A bake with an AO or a material channel in the same job takes Cycles' pixels.
- A kept layout whose map came from FSS OptiX: the direct write follows that map, Cycles' bake differs from it by its
  own coverage (the yoke base: 9,447 px); not compared on a real master.
- The panel and the report line in a live Blender.

### Re-Run

Under `cpucap.exe 50`, one at a time (logs in `tests/_work/d101/`):

- **The floor master** on a copy of COCKPIT.blend and its floor master in `tests/_work/d101/ck/`
  (`measure_floor.py`; the object's saved albedo is white): Custom albedo Overwrite through Cycles (the switch), then
  written directly.
  - The three images of 20494 x 24049 px **identical** (0 pixels differ), the map unchanged in both (0 px differ).
  - Time per master: **Cycles 141.4 s** (its bake 95.9 s), **direct 43.3 s** (the map read and the value 2.4 s; the
    composition and the three images the rest); wall time of the whole job 150.3 s and 51.7 s.
- `test_custom_value_channels` alone: PASS (24.1 s); against Cycles' bake max 1 of 65535 (VM's metal), 0 elsewhere,
  on 782,925 px per image; maps 0 px differ; the mixed bake within 0.
- `test_ao_bake` whole: ALL AO BAKE TESTS PASSED (372.8 s), 48 PASS and 0 FAIL; `test_material_channels` and
  `test_vertex_color_channels` unchanged; the 160 reference cases and the 196 scene-rule Refreshes equal.
- `test_panels` not run: the panel is unchanged.

## Session 82: Local View Ignored by the Bake (Decision 102)

- **Date:** 20261123
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5
- **Base Commit SHA:** 440d7f4 (with Sessions 78 to 81 uncommitted); `fss_blender_tools` raised to **0.113.0**

### Why

The maintainer asked whether a bake started in local view darkens with only the objects shown there. It did: the
bake's targets and occluders (`_visible_meshes`, `bakeable_copy`) were tested with `visible_get(view_layer=...)`,
which takes the 3D view of the context when none is given, and with it the view's local view (and its local
collections and object type filters). A bake started from the sidebar in local view therefore dropped the occluders
outside it, while the Refresh at file opening (a timer, no 3D view) counted them: the masters baked there turned
Stale on reopening, and a bake of All in local view baked only the objects in it.

### Maintainer Decision

102. **The bake ignores local view:** a bake started in local view takes the same targets and occluders as outside
     it; to leave occluders out, the object's Custom occluders.

### Built

- `ao_bake_store.py`: `shown_names(view_layer)` walks the layer collections as Blender's sync does (a collection's
  and its layer collection's viewport hiding inherited by the children, the exclusion not: a child may be included),
  `shown(obj, view_layer, names)` adds the object's `hide_viewport` and `hide_get`; `_visible_meshes` and
  `bakeable_copy` use them. `ao_bake.selected_copies` computes the names once. A view layer never synced has no layer
  collection (and no objects): no names.
- The panel's selecting and highlighting operators (`branch_objects`, the Detected tree's Select, the highlight) keep
  `visible_get`: what they select is what the artist sees.
- `tests/test_ao_bake.py` `test_shown_without_view`; `docs/user/bake.md` (What Is Baked), `fss_blender_tools/README.md`,
  `tests/README.md`, `AGENTS.md`.

### Not Verified

- The local view itself: a background Blender has no 3D view, and the tests run in the background only. `shown()`
  reads no view, so a view's local view cannot change it; not tried in a live Blender.

### Re-Run

Under `cpucap.exe 50`, one at a time, after the maintainer's bake in the E2 cockpit had ended (logs in
`tests/_work/d102/`):

- `test_shown_without_view` alone: PASS; 400 states, 2,800 objects, `shown()` equal to `visible_get` on each (1,343
  shown, 1,457 hidden). Its first run failed on an excluded collection's included child (shown by Blender): the
  exclusion is not inherited, fixed as above.
- `test_ao_bake` whole: ALL AO BAKE TESTS PASSED (404.0 s), 49 PASS and 0 FAIL; the 160 reference cases and the 196
  scene-rule Refreshes equal.
- `test_panels`: ALL TESTS PASSED (16 PASS).
- Deployed 0.113.0 to E1 and E2 portable and the user's own Blender.

## Session 83: Soften at Flat and Thin Triangles (Decision 103)

- **Date:** 20261123
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5
- **Base Commit SHA:** 440d7f4 (with Sessions 78 to 82 uncommitted); `fss_blender_tools` raised to **0.114.0**,
  `fss_ao_copy.dll` rebuilt (interface version 6 kept)

### Why

The maintainer showed black and white speckles on the applied AO of `AIRBRAKE_PANEL` (E2 cockpit), along the ledge
round its base, and asked whether the bake's unwrap caused them. It did not: the master was smooth there; a re-bake
and a re-Apply kept them. On a copy (`tests/_work/d103/`):

- The ledge is one 25-sided n-gon (poly 481); Blender's fan splits it into 23 triangles, long slivers (150 mm by 3 to
  33 mm), some over corners in a line: flat triangles, their far corner 0 um off the edge.
- The real Apply on the copy wrote the speckles too (poly 481: 24 % of its texels under 0.02, 11 % over 0.98); with
  Soften Sharp Edges off none, at 1, 12 and all threads the same (no race).
- Soften took the flat triangles' edges as folds (a flat triangle's normal is no direction): 4 of the 47 folds near
  the ledge. At a concave fold the DLL reads each face's slope over a step of at most 0.9 of the face's height over
  the edge, floored at 1 nm: over a flat triangle the step was 1 nm and the slope, and the value, ran to 0 or 1
  (clipped).
- My first check (Apply from the master on two of the ledge's triangles, both smooth) missed it; the probes had also
  imported the portable Blender's deployed add-on instead of this repository's (its startup script enables it), the
  same code then. Later probes import through `test_ao_bake`.

### Maintainer Decision

103. **Soften is safe at flat and thin triangles, in the DLL and in Python:** a concave fold's slopes are read over a
     step of at least a tenth of the softening width, else left out (the half difference alone); an edge of a flat
     triangle (far corner under 1 um off the edge) is no fold.

### Built

- `native/ao_copy/fss_ao_copy.cpp` `fss_ao_copy_soften`: `SOFT_SLOPE_MIN_STEP` 0.1; a concave fold whose step
  `min(0.5 W, 0.9 min(hX, hY))` is under it adds `0.5 delta fade` only. Rebuilt with `build.cmd` under
  `cpucap.exe 50` (one compiler process).
- `ao_bake_core.py`: `SOFT_FLAT` 1e-6 m; `soften_context` skips an edge whose triangle's far corner lies closer to it.
- `tests/test_ao_bake.py` `test_soften_thin_faces`: a wall (AO 0.4) over a smooth concave corner to a floor (0.6), the
  corner's middle vertex making the triangle along it flat or 2 um thin. Flat: no fold at it, every value 0.4 to 0.6;
  thin: its fold kept and every value 0.4 to 0.6 (the old DLL: 0.0). With the flat triangle's folds kept
  (`SOFT_FLAT` 0) the values are recorded: 0.01 (it has no pixels of its own in the master, so the half difference
  reads none either): the DLL's rule alone does not cover a flat triangle.
- `fss_blender_tools/README.md`, `native/ao_copy/README.md`, `tests/README.md`, `AGENTS.md`. The artist guide is
  unchanged (Soften does what it says, without the faults).

### Not Verified

- The ledge in the maintainer's Blender after a re-Apply (on the copy only).
- Folds whose triangle is thin but over a tenth of the width (0.1 to 0.28 mm at 1 mm): their slopes are read as
  before, amplified up to about 3 times the difference over the step.

### Re-Run

Under `cpucap.exe 50`, one at a time (logs in `tests/_work/d103/`):

- The real Apply (AO, Selected `AIRBRAKE_PANEL`) on a copy of the re-baked master and the saved COCKPIT.blend
  (`run/`, `runapply.py`, `cmp.py`): poly 481 0 % under 0.02 and 0 % over 0.98 (before 24 % and 11 %), polys 24 and 38
  the same; 4,673 of the panel's 232,485 texels changed, each 0 or 1 before (polys 24, 481, 13, 38, 328, 44, 267, 12,
  50), 12,620 of the 12,637 changed outside the panel within 8 px of it (padding); softened px 32,323 to 14,349.
- `test_soften_thin_faces` and `test_soften_edges`: PASS; the C++ soften copies as recorded and bit-identical at 1,
  12 and 24 threads. With the committed DLL (md5 22042e26...) `test_soften_thin_faces` failed (thin: 0.0); the new one
  put back (md5 a69392aa...).
- `test_ao_bake` whole: ALL AO BAKE TESTS PASSED (405.0 s), 50 PASS and 0 FAIL; the 160 reference cases and the 196
  scene-rule Refreshes equal.

## Session 84: Find Bent N-gons (Decision 104)

- **Date:** 20261123
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5
- **Base Commit SHA:** 440d7f4 (with Sessions 78 to 83 uncommitted); `fss_blender_tools` raised to **0.115.0**

### Why

After Session 83 two dark spots stayed: black patches on `AIRBRAKE_PANEL` (faces 480 and 481) and odd shading on
`COCKPIT_DOOR_CTRL_PANEL` (face 10). Both are n-gons with many corners along straight edges, slightly bent. Blender's
split of such a face (polyfill, then beautify) makes slivers tilted far off the face; the bake reads their normals
and darkens them, and the viewport shades them wrong. The maintainer fixed one panel by cutting the n-gon into quads
and the other by aligning its vertices' height (flattening it); both re-baked clean. The maintainer asked for a check.

### Maintainer Decision

104. **A check, Find Bent N-gons,** in FSS Tools > Checks: it selects the objects (and faces) with an n-gon of 5 or
     more corners whose split has a triangle more than 30 degrees off the face's normal or without area. It changes
     nothing; the artist flattens or cuts the face.

### Built

- `checks.py`: `BENT_NGON_DEG` 30, `BENT_NGON_CORNERS` 5; `bent_ngons(view_layer)` reads each local mesh once
  (`calc_loop_triangles`, each triangle's cross product against its polygon's normal; NaN counts) and lists every
  user of a shared mesh; `FSS_OT_FindBentNgons` (Object Mode) selects the visible objects (first active) and the
  faces, reports faces and objects with the time, prints each object's faces on the console. The button after Find
  Faces Without UV Area.
- `tests/test_uv_pack.py` `test_bent_ngons`; `tests/test_panels.py` checks the button is drawn.
- `docs/spec/general-tools.md` GEN-015, `docs/spec/inventory.md`, `docs/user/fss-tools.md` (Find Bent N-gons),
  `docs/user/bake.md` (a pointer under Limits), `fss_blender_tools/README.md`, `tests/README.md`, `AGENTS.md`.

### Measured

Synthetic (`tests/_work/d105/synth.py`, `lift.py`): a 20-corner rim, 100 x 20 mm, 10 corners in a line per side.

- Random height noise of 0.001 mm or less: a clean split; 0.01 mm: slivers 90 degrees off the face.
- One corner lifted 0.2 mm: 89.9 degrees; lifted 0.05 mm: 0.4 degrees (the split stays clean). The threshold of 30
  degrees lies in the gap: the bad splits found were 89.9 to 165 degrees off, the clean ones under 1.

On a copy of the cockpit (`cockpit_check.py`, before the maintainer's fixes): 132 bent n-gons in 80 objects, 0.25 s;
among them `AIRBRAKE_PANEL` 480 and 481, `COCKPIT_DOOR_CTRL_PANEL` 10, `PEDESTAL_TO_CHECK_PANEL` 374, 376 and 377.

### Not Verified

- The button and the selection in the maintainer's Blender.
- Which of the other 129 faces bake visibly dark: not every bent n-gon is in a place the AO shows.
- n-gons of 4 corners are not checked: a bent quad splits along one diagonal, tilted at most by its bend.

### Re-Run

Under `cpucap.exe 50`, one at a time (logs in `tests/_work/d105/`):

- `tests/test_uv_pack.py`: ALL TESTS PASSED, 42 PASS; `test_bent_ngons`: the flat rim not found, lifted 0.2 mm
  found on both users of its mesh and both selected, a bent quad not found, flattened again `({}, 0)`.
- `tests/test_panels.py`: ALL TESTS PASSED, 16 PASS, `fss.find_bent_ngons` drawn.

## Session 85: Falloff Curve and AO Strength (Decisions 105 and 106)

- **Date:** 20261123
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5
- **Base Commit SHA:** 72cd203; `fss_blender_tools` raised to **0.116.0**, FSS OptiX rebuilt (scene version 2)

### Why

The maintainer asked how to lighten the AO. A shorter distance and the linear falloff were the only ways; a fade that
keeps contact shadows and fades the shading from farther parts was not possible, nor an overall lighter AO without a
re-bake. The curve names were proposed after Blender's proportional editing, which artists know.

### Maintainer Decisions

105. **The falloff gets a Curve,** Linear (the default, as before), Sharp, Smooth and Root, the weights of Blender's
     proportional editing: an occluder at t darkens by w(1 - t / d), w(f) = f, f^2, f^2 (3 - 2f), sqrt(f). Both bakers.
106. **Apply gets a Strength slider** (default 1.0) for the AO: the darkening scaled toward white, 1 - s (1 - v).

### Built

- `ao_bake_core.py`: `FALLOFF_CURVES`, `FALLOFF_CODE` (1 to 4), `falloff_weight`, `falloff_distances` (Cycles' steps:
  Linear the same floats as decision 53, another curve where the weight is (n - 1)/n ... 0, nearest first).
- `ao_bake.py`: `falloff_curve` (Bake box, under Distance Falloff and Steps, active with the falloff), `ao_strength`
  (Texture Target > AO, under Soften Sharp Edges).
- `ao_bake_store.py`: `falloff_curve(scene)`; `falloff_curve` in the surroundings key (and a shared mesh's) only for a
  curve other than Linear with the falloff on, so earlier masters keep their hashes; the item and the job carry it.
- `ao_bake_runner.py`: Cycles' distances from `falloff_distances`, the log names a curve other than Linear; FSS
  OptiX gets the curve's code; the entry records `falloff_curve` (none for Linear).
- `ao_bake_native.py`: `SCENE_VERSION` 2, the header's falloff the code (0 off).
- FSS OptiX (`F:\glider`, uncommitted there; backups in its `.cache/backup/`): `AoBakeProtocol.h` `sceneVersion` 2,
  `falloffCurves` 4; `AoScene.cpp` refuses a falloff above 4; `device/AoBake.cu` `falloffLight` (Linear returns
  t / d itself, as before); `AoBakeLaunch.h` comment. Rebuilt (`aobaker.vcxproj`, Release x64, under `cpucap.exe 50`,
  `-m:1`, `CL_MPCount=12`), copied by `tools/update-ao-baker.ps1` (VERSION: glider b1aa064 with uncommitted changes,
  which include another task's image-viewer edits in Glider's core).
- `ao_bake_apply.py`: the strength applied to the AO's values after Soften (inside and padding), not to curvature or
  material channels; the report's line `strength s: the darkening scaled toward white`.
- Tests: `test_falloff` (Sharp and Root after Linear), `test_falloff_distances`, `test_apply_separate` (Strength 0.5
  and 0), `test_ao_baker_static.py` (`corner-falloff-sharp`, `-smooth`, `-root` against `curve_value`).
- `docs/user/bake.md`, `fss_blender_tools/README.md`, `tests/README.md`, `AGENTS.md`.

### Not Verified

- The panel in the maintainer's Blender, and a curve on a production master.
- Cycles' few steps follow a curve only as a staircase over it (at most 1/n darker); FSS OptiX follows it exactly.
  At 3 steps the two differ more than with Linear (O1 below: 0.7537 against 0.7190 for Sharp).
- The other FSS OptiX test scripts (`test_ao_baker_scenes.py`, `_cycles`, `_curvature`, `_denoise`) were not re-run;
  they pass the falloff as before (True is code 1).

### Re-Run

Under `cpucap.exe 50`, one at a time (logs in `tests/_work/d105/`):

- FSS OptiX built: `aobaker.vcxproj` Release x64, 0 warnings, `AoBake.cu` compiled to OptiX-IR (`build_aobaker.log`).
- `tests/test_ao_baker_static.py` with the new `aobaker.exe`: all 32 checks PASS (`static.log`). The corner against
  each curve's reference, 55,520 pixels, bound 5 sigma: Linear max 0.25 sigma (as before), Sharp 0.15, Smooth 0.22,
  Root 0.72. The reference's table equals the closed form of Linear within 1.2e-7.
- `tests/test_ao_bake.py` whole (Cycles): ALL AO BAKE TESTS PASSED (415.6 s), 51 PASS; the 160 reference cases and
  the 196 scene-rule Refreshes equal (the curve's Refreshes in `rule_unrecorded`). O1's mean with 3 steps: Linear

  scaled values.
- `test_falloff` with FSS OptiX (`falloff_optix.py`, the suite as a module): PASS; Linear 0.7196, Sharp 0.7537, Root

- `tests/test_panels.py`: ALL TESTS PASSED, 16 PASS.

## Session 86: The Master's Layout Measured Against the Artist's UVs (No Decision)

- **Date:** 20261123
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5
- **Base Commit SHA:** 72cd203 with Session 85 uncommitted; no add-on change

### Why

The maintainer saw stair steps along contact shadows and hole rims, and streaks on a bevel, after Apply, and asked
whether islands turned straight in the master, or a master baked in the object's own UVs, would reduce them (the
hunch: the master's islands differ from the object's, so their texels "jump"). Measured on `STEEP_APPR_PANEL`,
`STEEP_APPR_PANEL_SCREW` and `PEDESTAL_SHELL` (all `MAT_STD_COCKPIT_PEDESTAL`, 4096 px).

### Method

On copies of COCKPIT.blend (`tests/_work/d106/`), the file's settings (FSS OptiX, Linear falloff in 5 steps, Auto
Distance), Soften off; each object baked and applied into its own 4096 px file in three layouts, through a probe
module the runner loaded for this run only (removed after; `exp_layout.py`, `run.py`):

- **Today's** layout;
- **Aligned:** each island turned to its longest hull edge instead of the smallest rectangle;
- **UV4:** the object's own UVs at 4 x the texture's pixels (islands from its UV seams, skirt at the UV borders).

The ideal: the UV4 master averaged over each texel's 4 x 4 pixels that a face covers (any face), what a master in
the object's UVs at master density gives. Each Apply against it per texel (`compare.py`): inside one face, across
several faces (an edge inside a UV island), partly covered (a UV island's border).

### Measured

| Object (texels) | Class | Today: mean, p99, share over 0.1 | Aligned | UV4 applied |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Steep appr panel (105,429) | inside (85,591) | 0.0002, 0.002, 0 % | the same | 0.0001, 0.002, 0 % |
| | edge (17,109) | 0.0105, 0.286, 2.3 % | the same | 0.0106, 0.288, 2.4 % |
| | border (2,729) | 0.0041, 0.024, 0.2 % | the same | 0.0035, 0.021, 0.2 % |
| Screw panel (1,460) | inside (401) | 0.0008, 0.010, 0 % | the same | 0.0006, 0.009, 0 % |
| | edge (865) | 0.0219, 0.226, 7.6 % | the same | 0.0219, 0.225, 7.5 % |
| | border (194) | 0.0173, 0.141, 3.6 % | the same | 0.0162, 0.133, 3.6 % |
| Pedestal shell (1,659,348) | inside (1,489,581) | 0.0001, 0.001, 0 % | the same | 0.0001, 0.001, 0 % |
| | edge (154,254) | 0.0076, 0.164, 2.2 % | the same | 0.0072, 0.159, 2.1 % |
| | border (15,513) | 0.0063, 0.110, 1.1 % | 0.0063, 0.108, 1.1 % | 0.0064, 0.111, 1.1 % |

- Neither layout changes the result: inside a face every Apply equals the ideal within the ray noise, also where
  Apply reported faces under 4 x (133 of 217 on the panel; the master's density is 4 x the area-weighted median).
- The differences are at texels across several faces, the same in a master in the object's own UVs: Apply gives
  such a texel the average of its owner face's samples only (the face at the texel's centre), the ideal averages
  every face in it. In the crops (`crops_*.png`) they are one texel at a fold or rim, of the same pixel steps.
- The stair steps are in the ideal as well: they come from the texture's density on those faces (the shell about
  1.2 px/mm, 0.83 mm per texel; the steep approach panel 4.3 px/mm; the screw panel 1.7 px/mm).
- The real texture (`MAT_STD_COCKPIT_PEDESTAL_COMP.png`, written before this run) is lighter than this bake by 0.03 on
  the shell and 0.06 to 0.10 on the panels: applied from masters of other settings; not used for the comparison.

### Not Verified

- The bevel's streaks of the maintainer's screenshot: not located in the texture; the shell's worst windows were the
  holes' rims.
- Whether averaging every face of a texel (the ideal) looks better in the simulator than the owner face's average.

## Session 87: Object UV Masters, Blended Edge Texels and the Edge Filter (Decisions 107 to 109)

- **Date:** 20261124
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar, Claude Opus 5.5
- **Base Commit SHA:** 72cd203 with Sessions 85 and 86 uncommitted; `fss_blender_tools` raised in steps to
  **0.120.0**, `fss_ao_copy.dll` version 8

### Why

The maintainer showed aliased pixels on a screw hole's rim after Apply (texels small against the hole) and asked
whether they come from Apply choosing one master island per texel, and whether a master in the object's own UVs
would blend naturally. Session 86 had judged the layouts by their averages over all texels and called the difference
small; the maintainer's screenshots of a master baked in the object's UVs showed the rim interpolated where the
Auto Unwrap master stepped. The cause is Apply's rule: a texel takes the samples of its owner face only (the last
face covering its centre), so a texel across several faces jumps from one face's value to the next. The ideal of
Session 86 (every face of a texel averaged) is what a master in the object's UVs gives.

### Maintainer Decisions

107. **Master Layout:** a Bake setting, **Auto Unwrap** (default, the layout as before) or **Object UV**: the master
     in the object's own UV map at 4 x 4 master pixels per texel, its UV islands packed in rows (whole texels, not
     turned) so no master pixels are wasted; Apply writes each texel the mean of its 16 master pixels. Object UV stays
     beside the blend of decision 108: it is needed where the artist's UVs stretch or distort on purpose. Islands
     turned to the axes in the Auto Unwrap layout were set aside ("forget about rotated island first").
108. **Apply blends texels across several faces** of an Auto Unwrap master: each sub-sample reads the face that
     covers it, not the owner face, at no fewer than 4 sub-samples per axis. The target: the same rim close to the
     Object UV master's.
109. **Edge Filter** in Apply (Texture Target > AO), **Box** (default: every sample of a texel counts the same) or
     **Adaptive**: only on texels whose samples lie on two or more faces, a centre-weighted filter whose shape follows
     the sharpest fold between those faces, as a multi-sample anti-aliasing filter: equal weights on a flat join, a
     full tent at 90 degrees or more. The default stays Box until the maintainer has compared them.

### Built

- `native/ao_copy/fss_ao_copy.cpp` (`FSS_AO_COPY_VERSION` 8): `copy_inside(..., blend, adaptive)`. With the blend
  every candidate triangle of the bin cell is collected (not only those after the owner), each sub-sample finds the
  last one covering it and reads it (`read(f, bary, need = true)`), and all covered samples are averaged. Adaptive:
  where the samples lie on at least 2 faces, s = min(1, acos(the smallest dot of their normals) / 90 degrees), each
  sample weighted (1 - 2 s |dx|)(1 - 2 s |dy|), dx and dy its offset from the texel's centre in texels. Exports
  `fss_ao_copy_inside` (as before), `fss_ao_copy_inside_blend` (version 7) and `fss_ao_copy_inside_filter` (filter 0
  or 1, else `E_ARGS`). Rebuilt with `build.cmd` under `cpucap.exe 50`.
- `ao_bake_copy.py`: `VERSION` 8, `inside(..., blend, edge_filter)` picks the export.
- `ao_bake_core.py`: `APPLY_BLEND = True` (the tests of the locked references set it False), `BLEND_MIN_LEVEL` 4,
  `EDGE_FILTERS`; `copy_master(..., blend, edge_filter)` raises the level to 4 when blending, its info names both.
  `OBJECT_UV_SCALE` 4; `object_uv_layout` (UV islands by union-find over edges with equal UVs, shelf-packed in whole
  texels with a one-texel margin, the skirts as in `build_master`: the neighbour across the edge, or the face mirrored
  at a fold over `FOLD_SPLIT` or an open edge; degenerate triangles get master UVs 0); `object_uv_offsets`;
  `object_uv_box` (per island, in windows of 32 rows: each texel's covered master pixels averaged).
- `ao_bake.py`: `master_layout` (Bake box, under the falloff's Curve), `edge_filter` (Texture Target > AO, under
  Strength).
- `ao_bake_store.py`: an object whose baked faces are in one texture set, with Object UV on, gets `master_layout` in
  its surroundings key with the SHA-1 of those faces' UVs and the texture's size, so a UV or size change makes its
  master **Surroundings changed**; objects over several texture sets stay on Auto Unwrap.
- `ao_bake_runner.py`: `layout_for` builds the Object UV layout from the texture set's pixels; a stored layout is kept
  only when it equals the new one, and an Object UV layout is dropped when the setting is back on Auto Unwrap. The
  master's meta records `object_uv` (scale, per-island offsets) and its density.
- `ao_bake_apply.py`: `_object_uv_box_ok` (the master's UVs against the object's now); Object UV texels from
  `object_uv_box`, copied face by face with a warning when the UVs moved since the bake; the report lists the Object
  UV masters. Soften does not act on Object UV texels; the Edge Filter does not apply to them yet (they average
  equally).
- Tests: `test_object_uv` (`tests/test_ao_bake.py`: the packed offsets, 2,500 inner texels within 0.5 of 65,535 of
  the mean of their 16 master pixels, a moved UV making the master stale with Apply's warning, back to Auto Unwrap);
  `test_blend` and `test_edge_filter` (`tests/test_ao_copy_native.py`: a fold row at 9 sub-samples per axis).

### Measured

On the copies of Session 86 (`tests/_work/d106/`, FSS OptiX, Linear falloff in 5 steps, Soften off), the texels across
several faces against the ideal of Session 86 (`compare_packed.log`, `compare_ab.log`, `compare_adapt.log`):

| Object (texels across faces) | Today: mean, p99, share over 0.1 | Object UV | Blend (Box) | Blend, Adaptive |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Steep appr panel (17,109) | 0.0105, 0.286, 2.33 % | 0.0001, 0.001, 0 % | 0.0020, 0.050, 0 % | 0.0030, 0.066, 0.56 % |
| Screw panel (865) | 0.0219, 0.226, 7.63 % | 0.0002, 0.001, 0 % | 0.0066, 0.060, 0 % | 0.0078, 0.093, 0 % |
| Pedestal shell (154,254) | 0.0076, 0.164, 2.18 % | 0.0001, 0.001, 0 % | 0.0012, 0.021, 0.03 % | 0.0019, 0.046, 0.06 % |

- The blend brings the Auto Unwrap master close to Object UV at the rims without a re-bake (Apply only).
- Adaptive departs from the ideal by design (the ideal weighs a texel's faces equally): its largest difference from
